Important Notice
I.      The Board, the Directors and senior management of the Company warrant the truthfulness, accuracy and
        completeness of the information contained in this annual report and there are no false representations,
        misleading statements contained herein or material omissions herefrom, and jointly and severally accept legal
        responsibility.

II.     All Directors attended the Board meeting in relation to approve, among others, the results for the year ended 31
        December 2025.

III.    The consolidated financial statements of the Group for the Reporting Period prepared in accordance with the
        PRC GAAP and the IFRSs have been audited by Ernst & Young Hua Ming LLP (domestic auditor) and Ernst &
        Young (overseas auditor) respectively with standard unqualified audit report issued.

IV.     The person in charge of the Company, Mr. Zheng Gaoqing, the person in charge of accounting, Mr. Yu
        Minxin, and the person in charge of accounting department (accounting chief), Mr. Bao Xiaoming, warrant the
        truthfulness, accuracy and completeness of the financial report as set out in this annual report.

V.      The Board resolved to approved the profit distribution plan or plan of conversion of capital reserve to share
        capital for the Reporting Period

        The Board has recommended the final dividend plan for 2025 as follows: On the basis of the interim profit
        distribution plan of a cash dividend of RMB0.4 per share (tax inclusive) that has already been implemented for
        the first half of 2025, a final cash dividend of RMB0.6 per share (tax inclusive) is proposed for 2025, representing
        an aggregate cash dividend of RMB1.0 per share (tax inclusive) for the year 2025. The Board did not recommend
        conversion of capital reserve to share capital or issue of bonus shares.

        If, during the period between the date of disclosure of the Company’ s annual results announcement for the year
        ended 31 December 2025 to the record date for entitlement distribution, the total share capital of the Company
        or the number of shares held in the special repurchase securities account changes, the total distribution amount
        shall be adjusted based on the total share capital on the record date for future implementation of the distribution
        plan deducted by the number of shares in the repurchase dedicated securities account, in accordance with the
        principle that the distribution amount per share shall remain unchanged, and further announcement will be made
        regarding the specific adjustments.

VI.     Statement for the risks involved in the forward-looking statements

        This annual report contains forward-looking statements that involve future plans and development strategies
        which do not constitute a commitment by the Company to investors. Investors should be aware of the investment
        risks.

VII.    There is no misappropriation of funds by the controlling Shareholders and other connected parties for non-
        operation purpose in the Group.

VIII.   There are no external guarantees provided in violation of the stipulated decision-making procedures in the
        Group.

IX.     There is no more than half of the Directors that could not guarantee the truthfulness, accuracy and completeness
        of this annual report disclosed by the Group.

X.      Notice of major risks

        The Company has described the industrial risks in details in this report. Please refer to the content under the
        “Potential risks” section of “Discussion and analysis by the Company concerning the future development of the
        Company” in the “Management Discussion and Analysis” section of this report.

XI.     Others

        Unless otherwise specified, financial data involved in this report was extracted from the audited consolidated
        financial statements of the Group prepared in accordance with the PRC GAAP.

    Definitions to the frequently-used terms:

    Articles                         means      the Articles of Association of Jiangxi Copper Company
                                                Limited

    Board                            means      the board of Directors

    Chengmenshan Copper Mine         means      one of the five mines under production located in
                                                Jiangxi Province owned by the Company, located
                                                in Jiujiang City, Jiangxi Province, and also refers to
                                                Chengmenshan Copper Mine of the Company

    Company or Jiangxi Copper        means      Jiangxi Copper Company Limited

    copper cathode and refined       means      prefabricate thick plate with crude copper as anode
      copper                                    and sheet made of fine copper or stainless steel as
                                                cathode, and take the mixed solution of sulphuric acid
                                                and copper sulphate as electrolyte. After electrification,
                                                the copper is dissolved on the anode as copper ions,
                                                which then move from the anode to the cathode, pick
                                                up electrons and are deposited on the cathode

    copper concentrates              means      the concentrates from low grade ores containing
                                                copper achieving certain quality indicators through
                                                processing and dressing processes, which can be
                                                directly used for copper smelting in smelters

    copper contained in copper       means      the amount of copper in copper concentrates
      concentrates

    copper rods and wires            means      rods and wires made of copper cathode by melting,
                                                casting and rolling

    crude copper                     means      the raw copper materials including impure copper and
                                                blister copper

     Summary of Accounting Data and
     Financial Indicators


            Explanation on the differences between domestic and overseas
            accounting standards:

            Pursuant to the provisions of the Administrative Measures on the Appropriation and Use of
            Production Safety Fees of Enterprises ((2022) No.136) of the Ministry of Finance and the
            Ministry of Emergency Management of the PRC, appropriations of safety fees shall be made.
            The safety fees are used exclusively for the improvement and enhancement of enterprise
            production safety conditions. The appropriated safety fees are recorded as the cost or profit
            and loss for the period of the relevant products, and are separately reflected in “special
            reserves” under shareholders’ equity. Where the use of the appropriated production safety
            fees amounts to cost of expenditure, the special reserves shall be directly offset. Where
            the use of the appropriated production safety fees forms fixed assets, the costs incurred
            through the accumulation of construction-in-progress items are recognised as fixed assets
            when they reach their intended usable conditions after the completion of the safety projects.
            Meanwhile, the special reserves shall be offset by the costs of forming the fixed assets,
            and the same amount of accumulated depreciation shall be recognised. Such fixed assets
            shall no longer be depreciated in subsequent periods. Under the IFRSs, the production
            safety fees are individually reflected in the form of profit distribution in restricted reserve of
            shareholders’ equity when appropriated. Costs of expenditure within the prescribed scope
            of use shall be recorded in the statement of comprehensive income for the current period
            when such expenditures are incurred. Capital expenditures shall be transferred to property,
            plant and equipment upon construction completion, and shall be depreciated in accordance
            with the depreciation policy of the Company. Meanwhile, the actual amounts of, among other
            things, production safety fees used during the current period shall be carried over internally
            in shareholders’ equity, and shall be offset against restricted reserves and increase the
            undistributed profit, to the limit that restricted reserves are offset to zero.

      Note:    The Group has formulated a strict hedging plan and internal control system. Hedging transactions are only
               for the purpose of hedging the risk of changes in commodity prices and any speculative transactions are
               prohibited to safeguard the Group’s long-term stable development. For hedging transactions to which the
               Group has not applied the hedging accounting standards in the ordinary course of business, profits or losses
               from changes in the fair value of derivative financial instruments involved are recognised at the end of each
               period and transferred to investment profit/losses when the positions are closed, which is implemented in
               strict accordance with the requirements of the Explanatory Notice on Information Disclosure of Companies
               with Public Offering No. 1 – Non-recurring Profit and Loss, where the profits and losses arising from the
               above hedging transactions that are not accounted for by applying the hedging accounting standards in the
               ordinary course of business are included in non-recurring profits and losses.

I.    MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS

      Achievements of the 14th Five-Year Plan Period

      The 14th Five-Year Plan period was an extraordinary and remarkable five-year phase
      in the Company’s development history. Confronted with multiple challenges including a
      sharp decline in copper smelting and processing fees, violent fluctuations in commodity
      prices, and international trade frictions, the Company had stayed focused on the goals of
      “staying ahead, striving for excellence, and acting proactively”. It had steadily advanced all
      work around key tasks such as optimizing industrial structure, expanding resource layout,
      strengthening scientific and technological innovation, and improving the management
      system, achieving a series of major accomplishments and laying a solid foundation for
      building a world-class enterprise with global core competitiveness.


      (1)    Steady Improvement in Operational Quality and Efficiency


             During the 14th Five-Year Plan period, the Company had adhered to the high-quality
             development orientation, achieving effective improvement in quality and reasonable
             growth in quantity, with core operational indicators improved significantly. Operating
             revenue rose from RMB318.6 billion to RMB544.6 billion; the proportion of trading
             business income dropped from a peak of 49.20% to 26.66%, reflecting continuous
             enhancement in core business focus. Total profit increased from RMB3.336 billion
             to exceeding RMB10 billion for the first time, representing a notable strengthening
             of profitability. In the period, the Company had made forward-looking arrangements
             by establishing JCC Environment and integrating and setting up Jiangxi Copper
             Construction, further improving the full industrial chain layout and ensuring the
             sustained and sound development of the industry.




                                                                              2025 Annual Repor t      15
     Management Discussion and Analysis


           (2)   Continuous Optimization of Resource Layout


                 The Company had consistently pursued the development strategy of “industry-oriented
                 and resource-dominant”. Through diversified layout, it had made major breakthroughs
                 in resource cooperation and reserves. It had become the largest shareholder of
                 First Quantum Minerals Ltd. (“FQM”), participating deeply in the governance and
                 cooperation of world-class mining companies. It had launched a tender offer for
                 SolGold plc, expanding channels for resource acquisition. The Bakuta Tungsten Mine
                 in Kazakhstan and Phase III of Wushan Copper Mine had been completed and put into
                 production successively, and the Mexico slag processing project had been basically
                 completed, leading to a substantial expansion of resource reserves.


           (3)   Growing Innovation Strength


                 The Company upheld that science and technology constitute the primary productive
                 force, building a sound innovation ecosystem and focusing on core technological
                 breakthroughs and achievement transformation. During the 14th Five-Year Plan period,
                 the Company had won 2 national-level Science and Technology Progress Awards and
                 43 provincial and ministerial-level Science and Technology Progress Awards; added
                 252 new invention patents; established 6 national-level innovation platforms and 14
                 provincial-level innovation platforms; introduced and cultivated 12 leading talents and
                 302 doctors. The Company had led or participated in the formulation and revision of
                 127 standards, including 39 national standards and 88 industrial standards. It had
                 issued the Top-level Design of Digital JCC (《數字江銅頂層設計》), and more than 70
                 projects had been awarded or recognized as benchmarks at national and provincial-
                 ministerial levels, marking remarkable results in innovation-driven development.


           (4)   Increasingly Improved Management System


                 The Company had continued to improve the market-oriented operation mechanism of
                 “high growth and strong incentives”, established the performance-oriented assessment
                 approach of “performance speaks for competence”, and built a modern enterprise
                 management system featuring “scientific decision-making, efficient operation, and
                 strict risk control”. It had fully completed systematic reforms including the innovation
                 tackling campaign and value creation initiative. It had streamlined management
                 levels, promoted flat management of major job types and large teams, and improved
                 management efficiency. It had fostered 3 national-level green mines and 12 national-
                 level green factories, practicing the concept of green development.




16   Jiangxi Copper Company Limited
                Management Discussion and Analysis


Work in 2025

2025 marked the final year of the 14th Five-Year Plan period and the year for formulating the
15th Five-Year Plan. Centering on the development goal of “industry-oriented and resource-
dominant” and focusing on the main task of strengthening and optimizing the real industry,
the Company effectively responded to difficulties and challenges including intensified global
tariff competition, imbalance between supply and demand of copper concentrates, and
intensified competition in the copper processing industry. It advanced all work steadily,
successfully fulfilled the concluding tasks of the 14th Five-Year Plan, and laid a solid
foundation for the start of the 15th Five-Year Plan period.

In 2025, the Company recorded revenue of RMB544,623,000,000, representing a year-on-
year increase of 5.42% (the corresponding period of last year: RMB516,609,000,000; net
profit attributable to shareholders of the Company of RMB7,130,000,000, representing a
year-on-year increase of 2.41% (the corresponding period of last year: RMB6,962,000,000.
As at 31 December 2025, the Company’s total assets amounted to RMB218,691,000,000,
representing an increase of 13.24% as compared with that at the beginning of the year (the
beginning of the year: RMB193,128,000,000, of which net assets attributable to shareholders
of the Company amounted to RMB81,276,000,000, representing an increase of 4.27% as
compared with the beginning of the year (the beginning of the year: RMB77,945,000,000).

(2)   Improving quality and efficiency of industrial development


      In 2025, the Company continued to strengthen its competitive industries and deploy
      emerging sectors, advancing the high-quality development of the real economy.
      Projects including the upgrading of the copper-molybdenum separation flotation
      system at Dexing Copper Mine, the technical renovation and expansion of JCC
      Guoxing’s 260,000-tonne cathode copper project, the comprehensive recovery of
      gold-bearing polymetallic ores at Humon Smelting, the capacity expansion of 500kV
      ultra-high voltage cross-linked cables at Jiangxi Cable, the Phase-I 30,000-tonne
      technical renovation and Phase-II 150,000-tonne cathode copper expansion at
      Hongyuan were successfully completed and put into operation. As a result, the scale
      of the competitive industrial cluster was further expanded and its competitiveness
      continuously enhanced. Meanwhile, projects such as East China lithium battery
      copper foil, Shangrao 10,000-tonne-per-annum photovoltaic ribbon, and high-
      efficiency permanent magnet motors gradually ramped up production. The Company
      actively seized market opportunities in emerging industries including new energy
      vehicles, semiconductor packaging, and AI computing power, fostering new profit
      growth drivers.


      Mining and smelting entities fully capitalized on the favorable high copper price
      environment. Adhering to the principle of “utilizing both high-grade and low-
      grade resources to the full extent”, the Company achieved historic highs in key
      techno-economic indicators including molybdenum recovery rate, sulfur recovery rate,
      and overall copper smelting recovery rate, with continuous improvement in resource
      comprehensive utilization efficiency. Processing and marketing entities focused on
      product iteration, management enhancement, and cost reduction and efficiency
      improvement, achieving steady profit growth amid fierce market competition. Trading
      and financial entities centered on and supported the core business, strengthened
      synergy across the industrial chain, and improved domestic and overseas resource
      allocation capabilities, providing strong support for the achievement of the Company’s
      production and operational objectives.




                                                                      2025 Annual Repor t      19
     Management Discussion and Analysis


           (3)   Continuing expansion of resource layout

                 The Company further deepened the “resource-dominant” strategy, accelerated the
                 expansion of resource deployment, and spared no effort to enhance resource security
                 capabilities. In 2025, the Company joined hands with FQM to conduct resource drilling
                 and exploration in Kazakhstan, striving for new breakthroughs in resource expansion.
                 The South America office was officially put into operation, and Saudi Arabia branch of
                 Jiangxi Copper Construction and Kazakhstan subsidiary of JCC Geological Exploration
                 were newly established, further extending the “front-end reach” of overseas offices
                 and broadening channels for overseas resource acquisition.

                 To ensure the smooth progress of overseas resource cooperation, the Company
                 dispatched the first batch of 32 core production and technical personnel to Central
                 South University for a specialized training program featuring “domestic professional
                 advancement plus overseas practical experience”. It accelerated the development
                 of two professional talent teams for resource exploration and merger & acquisition
                 operations, providing high-caliber talent support for the development of overseas
                 resource businesses. Meanwhile, the Company joined forces with six provincial
                 enterprises in Jiangxi Province to “expand globally as a group”, establishing a three-
                 tier platform company covering Jiangxi-Hong Kong-Zambia to coordinate information
                 acquisition and implementation of African mineral resource projects. Leveraging the
                 advantages of the core business, diversified subsidiaries “went global by boarding
                 ships”. Mining and metallurgical products such as lining boards, switch cabinets,
                 and disc casting machines gradually expanded from Central Asia and Africa to Latin
                 America and Europe, accelerating the transformation from serving the core business
                 to serving the entire industry.




20   Jiangxi Copper Company Limited
                Management Discussion and Analysis


(4)   Accelerating cultivation of new productive forces

      The Company fully cultivated and developed new productive forces and continuously
      consolidated the foundation of scientific and technological innovation. The Company
      comprehensively deepened the “Seven Ones” cooperation with Nanchang University,
      jointly tackled key scientific research projects, and actively cultivated major original
      achievements. New material products such as special copper alloy conductors,
      molybdenum-rhenium and its alloys, and high-performance grain boundary diffusion
      magnetic steel are accelerating process verification and pilot test maturation, rapidly
      integrating into the development of future industries such as low-altitude economy
      and robotics. In 2025, the Company added 117 new research and development (R&D)
      projects, among which 13 were national and provincial/ministerial-level projects,
      representing a year-on-year increase of 200%. Five projects were approved under
      the 2025 National Science and Technology Major Project; one project was approved
      under the Key R&D Program of the Ministry of Emergency Management; one project
      was approved for the “2030 Pioneer Project” Major Special Program of Jiangxi
      Province; and three projects were approved under the Key R&D Program of Jiangxi
      Province. In addition, two of the Company’s scientific and technological achievements
      were granted provincial/ministerial-level Science and Technology Progress Awards,
      and three achievements received the 2025 China Nonferrous Metals Industry Science
      and Technology Award. The Company cumulatively held 1,022 patents, including 315
      invention patents. It added 358 new patents during the year, representing a year-
      on-year increase of 80.8%. The Company has cumulatively led the formulation of 12
      national, industry and group standards in the intelligent manufacturing sector (9 have
      been officially issued, 1 is under development, and 2 have been approved for project
      initiation).

(5)   Empowering development and stimulating vitality through reform

      The Company deeply promoted the digital intelligence of industries and the
      industrialization of digital intelligence. Dexing Copper Mine was rated four-star in
      digital transformation maturity, one of only 9 enterprises nationwide. Guixi Smelter
      passed the national Intelligent Manufacturing Maturity Model (CMMM) Level 4
      assessment, the first in the national non-ferrous metals industry. Ten entities including
      Huadong Copper Foil were selected as provincial-level manufacturing digital
      transformation benchmarks (smart factories).




                                                                        2025 Annual Repor t       21
     Management Discussion and Analysis


                 The Company continuously promoted the green and low-carbon transformation of
                 industries. New energy-saving equipment such as air suspension blowers and rare
                 earth permanent magnet motors were scaled up for renewal and application, saving
                 more than 50 million kWh of electricity annually. The Company explored and formed a
                 mine ecological restoration model of “systematic ecological restoration + landscape
                 adaptation to local conditions + shared restoration achievements”, and completed
                 nearly 1,600 hectares of ecological restoration in total. By innovating the “government
                 plus Enterprise plus social capital” model, the Company continuously promoted the
                 reclamation of industrial and mining wasteland, revitalized the existing construction
                 land, and achieved a win-win situation of ecological and economic benefits.

           (6)   Continuously strengthening risk prevention and control

                 The Company carried out in-depth special actions for “dynamic clearance” of major
                 accident hazards, built a three-in-one safety supervision model of “daily inspection +
                 special supervision + reporting rewards”, and consolidated the Company’s long-term
                 safe and stable operation. By setting up part-time rescue teams for non-coal mines,
                 the Company further strengthened the mine emergency support line.

                 The Company built a modern enterprise management system featuring “scientific
                 decision-making, efficient operation and strict risk control”, focusing on preventing
                 and resolving various operational risks and hidden dangers. The construction of the
                 audit management system was successfully completed, multiple audit models were
                 developed, the identification of key risk points and control of high-frequency problems
                 were strengthened, and the system operation efficiency and audit management
                 effectiveness were significantly improved.




22   Jiangxi Copper Company Limited
                      Management Discussion and Analysis


II.   INDUSTRY OF THE COMPANY DURING THE REPORTING PERIOD

      In 2025, the central trading range of copper price benchmark rose significantly and remained
      strong with fluctuations throughout the year compared with 2024. The two interest rate cuts
      by the US in the second half of 2025 fueled expectations of rising inflation, stimulating a
      strong surge in copper prices. The copper price spread between COMEX and LME widened
      considerably in the second half of 2025, prompting traders to carry out cross-market
      arbitrage to the US market, resulting in large volumes of copper being shipped to the US.
      This exacerbated the tight supply of copper in regions outside the US, and supported the
      sustained upward movement of copper prices. In terms of mines, affected by mine accidents
      at several major leading mines, Kamoa and Grasberg, copper ore supply decreased in the
      second half of 2025, and the weakened supply side provided impetus for the rise in copper
      prices. In December 2025, the SHFE copper price rose to a high of RMB98,000 per tonne,
      while the LME copper price reached a high of over US$12,000 per tonne. The average
      annual copper price in 2025 stood at RMB80,695 per tonne, up 7.52% from the average
      price in 2024. The average annual price of LME copper (3-month) was US$9,976 per tonne,
      up 7.63% from 2024.

        30                                         TC

             24-02   24-05    24-08    24-11    25-02    25-05     25-08    25-11    26-02



      In terms of fundamentals, on the raw material side, the full-year output of copper concentrate
      in 2025 recorded a year-on-year decline of approximately -2%, with supply from several
      large-scale mines experiencing declines. The spot prices of copper smelting treatment
      charges (TC) remained in the range of US$-50 to US$-40 per tonne in the second half
      of 2025, reflecting a shortage of copper ore. In terms of refined copper, global visible
      inventories reached 913,700 tonnes as of the end of 2025, an increase of 427,000 tonnes for
      the whole year. The continuous rally in copper prices in the second half of 2025 dampened
      downstream demand, which in turn drove the build-up of inventories. In terms of downstream
      industry demand, the growth rate of global copper consumption was 2% in 2025. Among
      them, the photovoltaic and new energy vehicle industries were the main contributors to
      copper consumption, while demand from traditional industries remained under pressure. In
      addition, the domestic real estate industry continued to underperform, with cumulative year-
      on-year downward trend in both new construction starts and completed floor area.




                                                                             2025 Annual Repor t       23
     Management Discussion and Analysis


           In terms of copper processing, China’s domestic copper processing industry overall
           presented a landscape of modest growth in total volume and structural divergence in 2025.
           According to data from the China Nonferrous Metals Fabrication Industry Association, the
           annual output of copper processed products reached 21.54 million tonnes, a year-on-
           year increase of 1.4%. Under the statistical caliber of the National Bureau of Statistics, the
           total output of copper products stood at 24.814 million tonnes, representing a year-on-year
           growth of 4.7%. In terms of segmented product categories, output of traditional copper rods,
           copper strips and ordinary copper tubes declined amid sluggish demand from the property
           sector, traditional industries and other related segments. Copper foil achieved growth driven
           by industries including the new energy sector, with particularly rapid growth recorded for
           lithium battery copper foil. In terms of processing profitability, the processing of ordinary
           copper products was plagued by overcapacity and intensifying cut-throat competition within
           the industry, while the gross profit margin of high-end copper product processing remained
           relatively robust. In addition, copper prices traded at an elevated level throughout 2025,
           further compressing the profit margins of some processing enterprises.

           In terms of policies, in accordance with the policy planning outline of the “15th Five-Year
           Plan”, the State will increase investment in power grids, focusing on smart grids, UHV power
           grids and rural distribution grids, with a projected growth rate of 9% during the “15th Five-
           Year Plan” period, which will provide support for copper demand. According to the Work Plan
           for Stable Growth of the Non-ferrous Metals Industry (2025–2026), the target average annual
           growth rate of domestic output is about 1.5%. The plan aims to improve resource recovery
           rates, advance project construction in an orderly manner, accelerate the review of mineral
           resource development projects, promote the commissioning of projects under construction,
           capacity expansion of operating projects and construction of new projects, while enhancing
           the level of metal application to support the development of industries such as artificial
           intelligence, low-altitude economy and aerospace. According to the Implementation Plan
           for High-quality Development of the Copper Industry (2025–2027), the target growth rate of
           domestic copper ore resources is 5%-10%, the utilisation efficiency of recycled copper will
           be improved, and the new capacity of copper smelting projects will be controlled. Therefore,
           the demand for recycled copper will increase, providing strong support for copper prices.

       state-owned asset management institution) as a single customer for
       aggregated disclosure. The Company considers suppliers under the
       control of the same controller (excluding those solely under the actual
       control of the same state-owned asset management institution) as a
       single supplier for aggregated disclosure.

                       (8)      Gearing ratio

                                As at 31 December 2025, the gearing ratio of the Group was 47.57%. The
                                gearing ratio is calculated based on net debt divided by total equity plus
                                net debt, where net debt is the sum of interest-bearing bank borrowings,
                                lease liabilities, accounts payable and notes payable, financial liabilities
                                included in other payables and accrued expenses, and corporate bonds,
                                minus cash and cash equivalents.

      Derivative investments

      (1)    Derivative Investments for hedging purposes during the Reporting Period

             Not applicable

      (2)    Derivative Investments for speculative purposes during the Reporting
             Period

             Not applicable

             Other explanations

             The Group’s product prices are mainly determined with reference to the
             prices of related products listed on the LME and the Shanghai Metal
             Exchange. Major raw materials and products, including copper, gold
             and silver, are important trading varieties in the international non-ferrous
             metal market and have their own pricing mechanisms in the international
             market. Due to the scarcity of resources of copper, gold and silver
             metals, the prices of copper, gold and silver metals are highly volatile,
             as they are affected by various factors, including global economy, the
             relationship between supply and demand, market expectations and
             speculations. Further, the purchase and sales pricing have a mismatch
             in supply-demand structure, cycle, pricing methods and pricing period,
             and the metal and mineral products market may experience significant
             fluctuations in the short term; therefore, the Group is subject to
             commodity price risks.




                                                                  2025 Annual Repor t       51
     Management Discussion and Analysis


                              The Group hedges against the price fluctuation risks by matching the
                              purchase and sales pricing, and adopts commodity futures contracts,
                              T+D contracts, option contracts, provisional pricing arrangements and
                              other derivative instruments based on the purchase and sales pricing to
                              mitigate the exposure to the commodity price risks, fully leveraging the
                              high correlation of the spot market and the futures market and reducing
                              the impact of commodity price fluctuations on the Group’s production
                              and operation. The Group develops a strict hedging plan and an internal
                              control system, under which hedging activities only aim to mitigate the
                              commodity price fluctuation risks and prohibit any speculation, so as to
                              ensure long-term and robust development of the Group.

                              For the said derivative investments including commodity futures
                              contracts, T+D contracts, option contracts and provisional pricing
                              arrangements that comply with the accounting requirements of
                              Accounting Standards for Business Enterprises No. 24 – Hedging, the
                              Group adopts the corresponding accounting treatment in accordance
                              with the relevant hedging accounting requirements and provides
                              disclosure in Notes X.3 of the consolidated financial statements. For
                              other hedging transactions for which the Group has not applied the
                              hedging accounting standards, profit or losses from changes in the
                              fair value of derivatives involved are recognised at the end of each
                              period and transferred to investment profit/losses when the positions
                              are closed. The amount of the said profit/losses from changes in the fair
                              value or investment profit/losses is affected by the quantity of relevant
                              commodities, the quantity of derivative contracts and the relevant price
                              fluctuations. The Group’s hedging transactions for which the hedging
                              accounting standards have not been applied and their financial effects
                              are disclosed in Notes V, 3, 28, 59 and 60 of the Group’s consolidated
                              financial statements.

     IV.   DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS BY THE COMPANY CONCERNING
           THE FUTURE DEVELOPMENT OF THE COMPANY

           (I)    Analysis of industry operational information

                  Please refer to “Management Discussion and Analysis: II. Industry of the Company
                  during the Reporting Period” for further details.

           (II)   Landscape and trend of industry

                  1.    Supply pattern. In 2025, the disturbance rate of the global copper ore supply
                        side increased significantly, and output growth fell short of expectations.
                        Affected by factors including production adjustments at some leading mines,
                        QB2 and other projects, the International Copper Study Group (ICSG) revised
                        down the global copper ore output growth rate to 1.4% for the whole year.
                        Policy disturbances, community relations and declining ore grades persisted
                        in major producing regions such as South America and Africa, the progress
                        of new projects was slow, and the resource monopoly pattern was further
                        consolidated. The tight supply at the mine side was deeply transmitted to the
                        smelting side. The copper concentrate treatment charge (TC) turned negative
                        in February and remained at a low level of around US$-40 per tonne for the
                        whole year, putting smelters under severe cost pressure. According to the
                        National Bureau of Statistics, China’s cumulative electrolytic copper output in
                        2025 was 14.72 million tonnes, a year-on-year increase of 10.4%. However,
                        small enterprises faced operational difficulties under profit squeeze, and the
                        trend of industry integration intensified.




54   Jiangxi Copper Company Limited
        Management Discussion and Analysis


2.   Demand pattern. Copper demand saw structural changes in 2025. In emerging
     fields, the production and sales of new energy vehicles maintained rapid
     growth, with both registering a year-on-year growth rate of over 20%, and
     copper consumption per vehicle was significantly higher than that of traditional
     fuel vehicles. The explosion of AI computing power accelerated the construction
     of data centers, and copper demand from data centers is expected to rise
     in the next five years. The structure of power investment was optimised, and
     the State Grid’s annual fixed asset investment hit a record high. In traditional
     fields, the real estate market remained sluggish, the housing starts showed no
     obvious recovery, and real estate-related copper consumption demand was
     weak. Overall, demand in the new energy and power sectors provided effective
     support, offsetting the downward pressure on demand in traditional fields.

3.   Copper prices. In 2025, copper prices generally showed a strong and wide-
     ranging fluctuation pattern, with the price benchmark rising significantly
     compared with 2024. The main fluctuation range of SHFE copper was
     RMB75,000-90,000 per tonne; the main fluctuation range of LME copper
     price was US$8,500-12,500 per tonne. In terms of driving factors, frequent
     disturbances at the mine supply side strengthened expectations of tight supply;
     on the macro level, the Federal Reserve started an interest rate cut cycle in
     September, and loose liquidity supported the financial attribute of copper.
     Uncertainty in US tariff policies triggered a global shift of copper flows to the
     United States, COMEX inventories rose while LME and SHFE copper inventories
     remained low, and structural imbalance intensified price fluctuations.

4.   Industry policies and competitive patterns. 2025 marks the full implementation
     year of the Implementation Plan for High-quality Development of the Copper
     Industry (2025–2027). Policies focus on improving raw material security
     capacity, standardising industrial order, and promoting green and intelligent
     transformation. New regulations require that new copper smelting projects
     should in principle be equipped with a corresponding proportion of equity
     copper concentrate capacity, no new smelting capacity will be added in key
     air pollution prevention and control areas, and the new smelting capacity in the
     year dropped to about 300,000 tonnes, a sharp decrease from the original plan.
     In terms of industry competition, large copper enterprises consolidated their
     market positions by virtue of resource and technological advantages, and small
     and medium-sized enterprises sought differentiated space in segmented fields.
     The industry is facing pressures from overcapacity and market competition,
     accelerating the survival-of-the-fittest process.




                                                               2025 Annual Repor t       55
     Management Discussion and Analysis


           (III) Development strategy of the Company

                 The Group will adhere to the strategic guideline of “focus on core business,
                 breakthrough in innovation, increase resource reserves, high-quality development,
                 march towards world-class”. It will fully promote the transformation and upgrading
                 of the industrial system, unswervingly consolidate, strengthen and optimise the real
                 economy, pursue excellence, and stride forward to become a world-class enterprise
                 with global core competitiveness.

           (IV) Business plan

                 Taking into account the current international and domestic macroeconomic
                 situation, the Company’s production and operation environment and development
                 situation, and the actual completion of production and operation in 2025, through
                 repeated discussions and adhering to the principles of seeking truth from facts
                 and being proactive, the Company’s production and operation plan for 2026 has
                 been determined as follows: production of 200,000 tonnes of copper concentrate,
                 2.39 million tonnes of copper cathode, 119 tonnes of gold, 1,413 tonnes of silver,
                 6.51 million tonnes of sulphuric acid and 2.07 million tonnes of copper processing
                 materials. The annual investment plan (including fixed assets investment and equity
                 investment) is RMB26.579 billion. (This operation target does not represent the
                 Company’s forecast for production in 2026. Whether it can be achieved depends
                 on various factors such as the macroeconomic environment and market demand
                 conditions, and there is great uncertainty. The Group will adjust the production and
                 operation plan in a timely manner according to market changes.)

(V)   Potential risks

      1.   Production safety risks

           During mining and dressing of ore and copper smelting, potential safety
           hazards may occur due to natural or human factors. Failure to detect and
           eliminate such factors in time will lead to major accidents, causing major
           property losses and environmental impact.

           In response to the risk of production safety, the Group will, as always, formulate
           and strictly implement a series of preventive measures suitable for the actual
           situation of the Company in accordance with national laws and regulations on
           production safety, strengthen production operation procedures and accident
           emergency rescue plans to avoid or eliminate losses caused to the Company
           by natural or human factors. At the same time, the main properties of the
           Company has been insured to reduce related risks and losses.




                                                                       2025 Annual Repor t       59
     Management Discussion and Analysis


                 2.    Exchange rate fluctuation risks

                       Imported copper raw materials purchased from international mining companies
                       or sizable trading companies by the Group and overseas investments are
                       generally settled in US dollars. With expansion of overseas business of the
                       Group, the income and expenses of foreign currencies would be even more
                       intense. Therefore, in case of more significant fluctuations in exchange rate or
                       failure to effectively control the exchange rate fluctuation risks by the Group,
                       it may result in exchange rate losses by the Group, which in turn may bring
                       certain negative impact on the profitability of the Group.

                       In response to the exchange rate fluctuation risks, the Company will closely
                       monitor the changes in national foreign exchange policies and exchange
                       rate information, enhance its ability to determine changes and trends of
                       the international exchange rate market to make prudent decisions, flexible
                       responses, and scientific grasp on the timing of raw material imports, the
                       choice of the country or region where the products are exported, and the
                       exchange rate hedging, so as to avoid the above-mentioned risks arising from
                       exchange rate fluctuations as much as possible.

                 3.    Risks from product price fluctuations

                       The Group is the largest copper cathode producer in the PRC and one of the
                       largest gold and silver producers in the PRC. The Group’s product prices are
                       mainly determined with reference to the prices of related products listed on the
                       LME and the Shanghai Metal Exchange. Copper, gold and silver are important
                       trading varieties in the international non-ferrous metal market and have their
                       own pricing mechanisms in the international market. Due to the scarcity
                       of resources of copper, gold and silver metals, the prices of copper, gold
                       and silver metals are highly volatile, as they are affected by various factors,
                       including global economy, the relationship between supply and demand,
                       market expectations and speculations. Price fluctuations will affect the revenue
                       and operating stability of the Company.




60   Jiangxi Copper Company Limited
        Management Discussion and Analysis


     In order to minimise the impact of product price fluctuations on productions
     and operations to the greatest extent, the Group intends to take the following
     measures to protect against risks from product price fluctuations: (1) closely
     monitor the trend of copper and gold prices in the international market,
     strengthen the analysis and research of various factors affecting the price trend
     of products, and take timely measures such as hedging to avoid risks from
     product price fluctuations; (2) the Group will take the world’s leading copper
     mines and smelting companies as benchmark, actively adopt new processes
     and technologies while improving management and operation efficiency, further
     reducing costs and expenses to resist the risks from product price fluctuations;
     (3) strengthen financial management level, enhance fund management, and
     reasonably arrange the raw material procurement and product sales of the
     Company to reduce the risk of significant tie-up of working capital of the
     Company due to rising product prices; and (4) strengthen the management of
     inventories and work-in-progress products, reduce inventories to the greatest
     extent to keep inventories at a reasonable level and reduce capital occupation.

4.   Risks from changes in the market environment

     The risks to the Company from changes in the market environment come from
     three aspects: (1) the development and operation of the macro economy
     directly affect total consumption demand, and the demand for the products of
     the Company will also alter according to the changes in the macro-economic
     cycle; (2) the demand from downstream market for products may change. For
     example, the market demand for copper products is mainly from consumption
     in the power, electrical, light industry, electronics, machinery manufacturing,
     transportation and construction industries. The development level and growth
     rate during different periods are imbalanced, and the demand for copper is also
     different, which will have a cyclical impact on the future business development
     of the Company; and (3) with the continuous improvement of research and
     production technology, the types and performance of relevant substitutes in the
     product application industry of the Company will continue to improve, which will
     have a direct impact on the product demand of the Company.

     In response to the risk from changes in the market environment, the Company
     will closely grasp the trend of the macro economy, pay attention to changes
     in related downstream industries, and strengthen industry research in order to
     further improve product quality and reduce production costs following changes
     in the market environment and actively develop new products that are more
     adaptable to market needs, and minimise the adverse impact of changes in the
     market environment on the operations of the Company to the greatest extent.




                                                               2025 Annual Repor t       61
     Management Discussion and Analysis


                 5.    Environmental protection risks

                       The Group is mainly engaged in the mining, smelting and processing of
                       non-ferrous metals and precious metals. In compliance with a number of
                       environmental protection laws and regulations concerning air, water quality,
                       waste disposal, public health and safety, the Group shall obtain relevant
                       environmental protection permits for its production and operation, and accept
                       inspections by relevant national environmental protection departments. In
                       recent years, the Group has invested a large amount of funds and technological
                       efforts in the transformation of environmental protection equipment and
                       production techniques, and worked on the treatment and discharge of
                       pollutants in accordance with national environmental protection requirements.
                       However, if the environmental protection department continues to raise the
                       environmental protection standard in the future, adopt more extensive and
                       strict pollution control measures, the Group’s production and operation may
                       be affected, leading to an increase in operating costs such as environmental
                       protection expenses.

                 6.    Risks from uncertainties

                       In the recent years, the Company’s resilience in production and operation
                       is being challenged amid the evolution of the global landscape intensified
                       by geopolitical conflicts, profound adjustments in the global energy market,
                       accelerated transformation of the energy structure, uncertain prospects of
                       the monetary policy of the US Federal Reserve, insufficient momentum of
                       recovery of the global economy, and numerous uncertainties to the economic
                       development of the world. The Company will fully analyse both domestic and
                       foreign situations, and refine the production and operation planning.

             Management Discussion and Analysis

    Boosting Efficiency, and Prioritising Shareholder Returns” Special Initiative for
    Shanghai Listed Companies, the Company formulated the 2025 “Enhancing Quality,
    Boosting Efficiency, and Prioritising Shareholders’ Return” Action Plan based on its
    actual operational situation and strategic objectives. The Company has consistently
    advanced its implementation, and hereby report the progress of the action plan as
    follows:

    1.    Focusing on core business to enhance quality and efficiency,
          strengthening the foundation and building strength

          Anchored in the development principle of “focusing on core business,
          achieving breakthroughs in innovation, pursuing high-quality development, and
          striving to become a world-class enterprise”, while strengthening and refining
          the non-ferrous metals business, high-quality supporting industries such as
          new materials, equipment manufacturing, and information technology along
          the value chain were emphasised. In 2025, the Company’s mining, smelting
          and processing production units improved efficiency and quality, tapped into
          potential to maintain steady growth, and production volumes of key products
          such as copper cathode, gold and sulphuric acid all achieved steady progress.
          In terms of project construction, the first overseas mine was successfully
          completed and put into operation, and a number of key projects were efficiently
          completed and put into operation.

    2.    Strengthening innovation-driven, and activating development momentum

          The Company established a technology innovation committee led by the
          Company’s top executives to comprehensively coordinate overall technology
          innovation work, built, revised and improved a series of technological research
          systems, and promoted the deep integration of technological innovation
          and industrial innovation. The green beneficiation reagent developed by the
          Company has completed industrial tests, which reduced beneficiation costs
          while greatly improving the recovery rates of various non-ferrous metals. In
          2025, the Company added 117 new scientific research projects, including 13
          projects at the national, provincial, and ministerial levels. As of the end of 2025,
          the Company held a total of 1,022 patents, including 315 invention patents. A
          total of 358 new patents were added during the year, representing a year-on-
          year increase of 80.8%.

III.   CORPORATE GOVERNANCE CODE

       The Company strives to maintain and establish high quality corporate governance.

       To the knowledge of the Board, during the Reporting Period, the Company had been in full
       compliance with all the code provisions under Part 2 of the Corporate Governance Code (the
       “Code”) as contained in Appendix C1 to the Listing Rules, with the exception as disclosed in
       this Corporate Governance Report and the following deviation:

       During the Reporting Period, legal actions which the Directors may face had been covered
       by the internal control and risk management of the Company. As the Company considered
       that no additional risk was likely to exist, insurance arrangements in respect of legal actions
       against the Directors had not been made as required under code provision C.1.8 under the
       Code.

       The followings are the corporate governance practices adopted by the Company:

       (1)   Shareholders and shareholders’ meetings

             The Company seeks to ensure that all Shareholders, especially minority Shareholders,
             are able to enjoy equal status and fully exercise their rights and perform the
             corresponding obligations effectively. Meanwhile, it seeks to ensure that they enjoy
             the rights to be informed of and participate in the Company’s significant events as
             stipulated under relevant laws and regulations and the Articles.

             The procedures for convening, holding of, considering resolutions and voting at the
             shareholders’ meetings of the Company are in strict compliance with the relevant
             regulatory provisions of the places where the Company’s shares are listed as well
             as the requirements of the Articles. All shareholders’ meetings of the Company are
             witnessed by the PRC lawyers with the representative from auditors as the scrutineer.




                                                                               2025 Annual Repor t       67
     Corporate Governance Report


           (2)   Relationship between the controlling Shareholder and the
                 Company

                 JCC, being the controlling Shareholder, performs its rights and obligations in
                 accordance with laws. The economic business between the Company and its
                 controlling Shareholder is carried out strictly in accordance with market and
                 commercial principles and follows the approval procedures for connected
                 transactions. The controlling Shareholder has not overridden the power of the
                 shareholders’ meeting of the Company to interfere directly or indirectly the operating
                 activities of the Company. The Company and its controlling Shareholder are
                 independent from each other in terms of business, assets, organisation, finance and
                 personnel. The Board and the internal functions of the Company are able to operate
                 independently.

           (3)   Directors, the Board and senior management

                 The Board is mainly responsible for devising the Company’s overall strategies
                 such as development strategies, management structure, investment and financing,
                 budget, financial control and human resources (including reviewing and monitoring
                 the training and continuous professional development of the Directors and senior
                 management personnel of the Company and formulating, reviewing and monitoring the
                 code of conduct and compliance manual of employees and Directors) and overseeing
                 the operations of the Company. The Board is also responsible for reviewing and
                 monitoring the policies and practices regarding the Company’s compliance with laws
                 and regulatory requirements and standardising the operations and disclosures of the
                 Company in accordance with the listing rules or other rules and regulations of places
                 where the shares of the Company are listed and reviewing the financial performance of
                 the Company. In addition, the Board is also responsible for formulating and reviewing
                 the Company’s corporate governance policies and practices and reviewing the
                 Company’s compliance with the Code and the disclosure in the Corporate Governance
                 Report during the Reporting Period.

                 Particularly, the chairman of the Company (the “Chairman”) leads and supervises the
                 operation of the Board and effectively plans Board meetings to ensure that the Board
                 acts in the best interests of the Company. Under the leadership of the Chairman, the
                 Board has adopted sound corporate governance and procedures and taken adequate
                 measures for efficient communication with Shareholders. The Chairman implements
                 the Board’s decisions and makes daily management decisions. The powers of the
                 Board and Chairman are set out in the Articles in details.

through establishment of formal and transparent procedures; to review and
approve proposals in respect of remuneration of the management in response
to the various enterprise principles and targets set by the Board; to propose
remuneration of all executive Directors and senior management which includes
non-monetary benefits, pension rights and compensation to the Board; to
provide advice to the Board in respect of the remuneration of the non-executive
Directors; to consider the remuneration paid by companies of similar type, time
and duties devoted as well as employment conditions of other positions within
the Group; to ensure that no Director or any of his/her associates determines
his/her own remuneration; and to perform other duties specified in the terms of
reference of the remuneration committee.

the time attended the meeting, and considered the “2024 Remuneration for
Directors, Supervisors and Senior Management, and Travel Allowances for
Independent Directors” submitted by the human resources department of the
Company. Subsequently, on 28 August 2025, the Board amended the "Rules
of Procedure for the Remuneration Committee of the Board of Jiangxi Copper
Company Limited". The remuneration committee shall review the proposed
remuneration distribution plan based on the progress in achieving the major
business objectives of the Company and the management positions and
responsibilities of the Directors and senior management, and submit to the
Board for review upon voting and passing of the same.

     Corporate Governance Report


           (6)   Board diversity policy

                 The Board has adopted a diversity policy for the Board members, and the nomination
                 committee is responsible for monitoring the measurable targets and the progress of
                 achievement as set out in such policy.

                 The Company understands and believes that the diversity of the Board members
                 can greatly benefit the enhancement of the quality of its performance. With a view
                 to achieving a sustainable and balanced development, the Company views the
                 increasing diversity at the Board level as an essential element in supporting the
                 attainment of its strategic objectives and sustainable development. All appointments
                 to the Board are based on meritocracy. Candidates are selected objectively, having
                 taken into full account the benefits of diversity of the Board members and based on
                 a series of diversity perspectives and measurable targets. These measurable targets
                 include but are not limited to gender, age, cultural and educational background,
                 professional experience, skills and knowledge. In addition, the Board reviews the
                 implementation and effectiveness of the Board diversity policy on an annual basis.
                 The nomination committee has performed the abovementioned duties, including
                 selecting candidates and reviewing the implementation and effectiveness of the Board
                 diversity policy. Having considered that the current proportion of the independent
                 non-executive Directors on the Board ranges from one-third to 44%, most Directors
                 are aged between 40 and 60, and the cultural and educational backgrounds of
                 the Directors cover various fields, such as mining, management, finance and
                 accounting, in which the Directors have made outstanding achievements, the
                 nomination committee is of the view that the current Board has fully demonstrated a
                 diverse composition in terms skills, experience, knowledge, cultural and educational
                 background and independence.

                 As at the publication date of this report, the tenth session of the Board comprised two
                 female members. The Company will continue to take into account the Board diversity
                 policy fully, and place emphasis on including gender as a factor for consideration for
                 the achievement of Board diversity of the Company.

                 The Company is also devoted to achieving gender diversity in the entire workforce. As
                 at the end of the Reporting Period, the number of female employees of the Company
                 accounted for approximately 15.3% of the total number of employees, which is a
                 relatively high ratio in the mining and smelting industry. The Board is of the view that
                 the Company has achieved gender diversity among its workforce. The hiring strategies
                 of the Company achieve diversity in the workforce, including the senior management,
                 in terms of gender, age, ethnicity, cultural and educational background, professional
                 experience, skills and knowledge, etc.




76   Jiangxi Copper Company Limited
                                      Corporate Governance Report


      (7)   Policy for nomination of Directors

            The procedures of appointment, re-election and removal of Directors are set out in
            the Articles. The nomination committee carefully considers aspects including the
            qualifications and biographies of Director candidates and then recommends them
            to the Board. After the Board passes the resolutions in relation to the candidate
            nomination, the resolutions will be submitted to the shareholders’ meeting of the
            Company for consideration and approval. The nomination committee and the Board
            will consider a number of factors in making nominations, including but not limited
            to skills, expertise, industrial experience, integrity, independence (regarding the
            independent non-executive Directors) and the diversity of the Board.


IV.   PEER COMPETITION AND CONNECTED TRANSACTIONS

      (1)   Peer competition

            During the Reporting Period, there was no substantive peer competition between the
            Company and its controlling Shareholder, JCC.

      (2)   Connected transactions

            The Company was established in 1997 through separation of part of the assets from
            the controlling Shareholder, JCC. Hence, certain connected transactions are inevitable
            between the Company and JCC and its subsidiaries (other than the Group) from time
            to time. Such connected transactions are in compliance with the market and business
            principles and follow the approval procedures for connected transactions.

            The Company has sought to reduce the connected transactions with JCC and its
            subsidiaries (other than the Group) from time to time since its listing. The types of
            connected transactions between the Company and JCC and its subsidiaries (other
            than the Group) from time to time have been substantially reduced due to the
            increasing acquisitions of assets of JCC and its subsidiaries (other than the Group)
            from time to time by the Company and the socialisation of part of the assets of JCC.

            For details of the connected transactions conducted between the Company and
            JCC and its subsidiaries (other than the Group) from time to time, please refer to the
            contents of “Material Connected Transactions” in the chapter of “Significant Events” in
            this report.

X.    SHAREHOLDERS’ RIGHTS

      The Company ensures that all the Shareholders enjoy equal rights and they can fully
      exercise their rights based on their shareholdings. The Articles expressly provides that the
      Shareholder(s) holding more than 10% (including 10%) of the issued shares with voting rights
      of the Company may request the Board to convene an extraordinary shareholders’ meeting
      in writing. The relevant procedures on convening, holding of and voting at the shareholders’
      meetings are in strict compliance with relevant laws and the Articles.

      Shareholders, individually or aggregately, holding over 1% of the shares of the Company
      shall have the right to propose provisional motions to the convener of the shareholders’
      meeting in writing ten (10) days prior to the shareholders’ meeting. The convener shall
      within two (2) days of receipt of the provisional motions issue supplemental notice of the
      shareholders’ meeting to disclose the contents of the provisional motions.

      The Articles also expressly provides that the Shareholders are entitled to supervise and
      manage the business and operation of the Company, put forward recommendations or
      questions, inquire relevant information as well as enjoy the rights to be informed of and
      participate in the Company’s significant events. For details of the procedures and methods
      of inquiry, please refer to the Articles. The Company values good communication with the
      Shareholders. The main communication channels of the Company include shareholders’
      meetings, the Company’s website and email, the facsimile and telephone of the secretariat
      of the Board as set out in the section headed “Corporate Profile” in this report, which are
      available for the Shareholders to express their opinions or exercise their rights.
